Some days, you had no context for what was going on in The Wandering Inn. Not ‘most days’; the inn was actually fairly predictable 73% of the time.
And yes, Yelroan had calculated it. He’d been out all day, checking on the election results and trying to predict how it would go.
He was conducting polls, another Earther invention—but they invented everything. He wanted to make sure his model worked using Liscor’s election as a trial run, and if he was right…the results might shock you.
However, he felt bad when he came back and saw Xinthe wearing a Shield Spider hat of shame and serving guests with it on her head.
“What’s going on with Xinthe?”
“Oh, hey, Yelroan. She’s being punished.”
“Ah, like normal, then? Anything else I missed, Peggy?”
It was astonishing to Yelroan how much he could hang out with the staff. Plain’s Eye had never really liked him like that, even though he was their tribe. That only hurt a lot in hindsight. Anyways, Peggy gave him a rundown. Yelroan’s eyebrows bobbed up.
“Ilvriss learned the magical dance? That’s ahead of my predictions.”
She swatted his arm playfully.
“Oh, you! You know everything, you smort guy, eh? No tricking you!”
Yelroan chuckled, embarrassed. To Peggy, the former Redfang, and everyone else, Yelroan was the ‘smart’ [Mathematician]. Even though he’d been to less school as a cub than even Ser Dalimont…he’d just gone to school later as an adult. You never stopped learning.
However, he was guilty because, well, he’d been out. He wasn’t needed at the inn. Oh, sure, he kept moving the money around, making it grow for the inn, but Yelroan had a secret.
He’d been giving money to the Plain’s Eye tribe and continuing to help Merish. His friend needed to establish something in the New Lands. His tribe was in danger.
Of course, Lyonette had approved giving money to Gnollish tribes—that wasn’t the secret. The secret was that Yelroan was, well, moonlighting. Selling his services to interested parties and using the proceeds on other projects and trying to help his former tribe.
He felt bad because he was an employee of The Wandering Inn. But something had occurred to him.
This place…doesn’t really need me. Yelroan sipped from a drink of goat’s milk as he leaned on the counter and watched Mrsha harassing Xinthe. It was the only time the girl looked like a girl, and he thought Xinthe put up with it because she knew what Mrsha had been through.
It had been Mrsha who’d called for Yelroan when Erin had needed help, and it was a good idea, but…a kid’s idea, if that made sense. Send for a [Mathematician] to run an inn’s finances.
It was like calling for a Terland Golem to farm a single field. Yelroan ran out of work. Make no mistake, he’d been needed—he’d fought some unforgettable battles when the [Box of Incontinuity] had first activated.
Glad I was here for that. I actually levelled. And Yelroan had his great work, his project to quantify magic. But most days, he was just…unneeded.
He’d been thinking about that a lot, really. He had a conclusion in mind he didn’t like, but his counterargument was: why leave?
The inn gave him free room and board. He loved it here. He could help with Mrsha, and when he was needed, his impact would be far greater than anywhere else. He said that to himself, but when the call came for him, he was still glad.
“Hey, uh, Peggy?”
“What? Inkpaper, if you’re slacking off, I hit you with my ass!”
Peggy turned, two pizza platters in each hand, and the lazier [Librarian] waved his claws.
“No, I was working! Someone calling the inn for [World’s Eye Theatre] call. Private. Very worried. She on the list. Qwera!”
Yelroan’s ears perked up. Peggy hesitated.
“Lyonette is napping. She very tired after dancing. Maybe it wait? Or it goes to Mrsha?”
They turned to the giggling Gnoll girl who’d created the Path of Penance with Nannette; an oil-slicked floor filled with obstacles that Xinthe had to navigate to serve food to a very worried series of guests.
“If she crashes, the next order’s free!”
“Maybe it waits?”
Peggy suggested, glancing around for Dalimont or Ushar or—Yelroan raised a paw.
“What about me?”
He was a ‘senior’ member of staff, and Peggy gratefully nodded. So the [Mathematician] took a slice of pizza and drink to the [World’s Eye Theatre]. He heard a crash and winced.
“What did Xinthe actually do?”
Asgra was sweeping the huge theatre with a broom. She shrugged.
“She put naked Ilvriss and Lyonette together. On purpose, I bet. She on team Ilvriss. Grr. I her rival.”
“Got it. Thanks, Asgra. Er…what team are you on?”
“Team Dalimont! He not want me on his team, but I know better.”
Yelroan decided that was largely harmless and not his business. He walked down, activated the Skill, and spoke.
“Can I get a very secure call to Merchant Qwera, please? Thank you. Qwera? Hello? Can you hear me? Can you establish privacy spells but let me through?”
“Who’s there? Yelroan? Tribes, thank goodness—”
It took a second for Qwera to appear as he squinted into a world of static. He entered a vision of Qwera’s surroundings, but then he found himself in one of her wagons. The image was a bit static-y, which he took to represent anti-scrying spells. Qwera appeared rattled. Some of her golden fur was brown, and she was panting for air.
She’s rattled.
“What is it, Qwera? An attack? Can I send word to anyone?”
The Golden Gnoll of Pallass was a generously filled woman with her iconic scintillating fur, a maven of business. He quite respected her acumen, and he’d never seen her this rattled, even at the Meeting of Tribes. Qwera looked around again.
“No. No. I just…I’ve been thinking on who to contact. I had to tell someone, even if it’s dangerous. I’ve deliberately not reached out for a while because I think she might have been monitoring who I’m calling for—Yelroan. I was trying to get the Silver Swords out of trouble. You know that?”
“Of course. I’ve been leaning on the Merchant’s Guild myself, but they’ve been unusually stubborn.”
He frowned. Lyonette had asked him to help, but something—internal politics—had overridden the usual levers of gold he could pull. Qwera nodded as her fur began to settle back. She started dyeing her fur distractedly.
“It’s someone new in the Guild. A Chandrarian—a member of Roshal. A [Slave Lady] just swept the Merchant’s Guild—she’s been altering their internal decisions worldwide! Do you know…a Slave Lady Andra?”
Yelroan did, if vaguely. He tracked new players in the game of commerce, and his fur rose as she related her encounter.
“Wait, she had a nine-way conference? Then a glowing Antinium exploded her?”
“She’s not dead. I hoped she was, but when I found out—she’s a ghost, Yelroan.”
“One of Roshal’s. I know. The Prophet Pawn attacked her. Damn, he didn’t get any of them, then?”
“The—the what?”
Qwera was completely taken aback, but Yelroan remembered the [Palace of Fates]. He drummed his paws on the counter.
“That’s not good at all. She’s probably Level 50, even with her level reduction, and she’s a ghost. With Roshal’s economic might? A [Lady of Coin] at her level could alter the world economy.”
“You don’t need to tell me that! I felt like she was squashing me, Yelroan. I want to work with her—and I swear I can’t tell if that’s because I want to gain her trust or if I’m being blown away by her class. I—someone needs to stop her.”
“I’ll tell Lyonette.”
Yelroan promised, but what could the [Princess] do? Roshal was not an enemy the inn wanted. Yet. Lyonette had hidden the inn because she was worried about them. The inn had a lot of power. The ability to take on the Slave State of Chandrar…no. And this was an enemy who fought with numbers and coin, which Lyonette, good as she was, was only middling in.
“Who could stop her?”
“The…Quarass? Wall Lord Ilvriss is no slouch, but he’s busy and outclassed. This sounds like a real nightmare.”
“One who has time to work and who no one is checking! Yelroan, only a top-tier [Merchant] would know she’s in the Guild Headquarters. She’s put policies into place that are going to—she’s going to have debtors enslaved. I can’t stop it! I’ve argued myself hoarse, and there are good [Merchants] who see what she’s doing, but you know what an oxymoron that is around here.”
Qwera was right to be worried. Yelroan devoured his pizza as he thought.
“I…I’ll help. I think I’ve already locked horns with one of that lot, and they’re dangerous. Listen, Qwera, can you stick around?”
“I’m not going to sleep tonight until I get something done, so yes! Ysara is also willing to help, but she’s trying to advocate for Ylawes. She can run our business, and I can do Andra’s errands if that helps—”
“You need to use that information or you’re just working for her, Qwera. And her class probably corrupts. In two months? You’ll start seeing it her way. Two years and your morality might shift until you’re on her side.”
The Golden Gnoll blanched.
“I’d rather eat a wand. That’s why…who do you have in mind?”
Honestly? Yelroan was so worried he paced around in a circle.
“Most of the Gnolls I’d have told—I’d have told Xherw and hoped he had someone. A lot of my allies are dead. And Fetohep of Khelt wouldn’t have the time and gold…”
They had lost a lot of allies. Qwera bit her lip, and Yelroan snapped his fingers. He gave Qwera his best grin to reassure her.
“There’s only one person in this world who can take Andra on. I know exactly who—let me adjust the receiver…I might have to petition for an audience, but…”
There was only one champion of this realm who could fight even Andra on equal terms, even if they might be outlevelled. One hero to champion Izril—no, the world.
 
——
 
Secretary Salii was slurping noodles out of a bowl in a war camp.
“…What?”
Two Gnolls were waving at her, one from a scrying orb, another just standing there. Salii knew The Wandering Inn of course. She just hadn’t realized Yelroan and Qwera were so deeply tied to it.
“That’s the situation, Secretary Salii. Izril needs the greatest [Secretary]. You’re up against someone who might outlevel you. At least, she was probably Level 60+ in life. And she’s got way more leverage. We can give you some, but you’re needed.”
The Gnoll’s sunglasses flashed, and Salii shaded her eyes with a claw.
“Um…listen. I appreciate the vote of confidence, and that actually sounds like fun. But I’m sort of employed, working for Pomle? We are at war, you know, and my father didn’t raise a quitter.”
She jerked her thumb at Orjin and the [Martial Artists] continuing their war for Pomle’s freedom. Yelroan hesitated.
“But Salii—”
“This Slave Lady is way more dangerous than Nerrhavia’s Fallen!”
Qwera broke in. Salii leaned back and adjusted the volume on the scrying orb.
“Yeah, I know. I’m not stupid. She sounds like a true menace. Damn Roshal. Working for the Demons was like the fifth option on my list of jobs. But without the Deathless, I thought it wasn’t worth it…I’d have considered going there if I’d known they’d come back.”
That sentence made Vandum walk straight into a tent pole and drew even the two Gnolls up. Salii rolled her eyes.
“I’m a mercenary of contracts, you two. I’m not the good guy you think I am. Mind you, I don’t care for [Slavers]—but I’m not going to pull up and start a counteroffensive in Izril. I don’t have any contacts, and frankly, I’m sick of Drake politics, and I’d have to be right back in there. Orjin’s refreshing. He’ll just punch someone in the face and skip two months of arguing.”
She smiled, and Yelroan spoke urgently.
“Secretary, I know you’re loyal, but you’re not blind. Without help, this will become a crisis, and Pomle is on Roshal’s chopping block for what it did.”
She smiled grimly.
“I know. And I’m not intending to do nothing. I just can’t devote my own time to it. I’ll make trouble here. Don’t you worry, I appreciate the heads up. But I have to leave this fight in more capable hands.”
“Whose? You have a name?”
She slurped more noodles and pointed a fork.
“Yep. You.”
She pointed at Yelroan, and Qwera blinked. Yelroan just held up his paws.
“Secretary, I’m flattered, but—”
“You’re the [Mathematician] who kept the largest tribe in Izril running by yourself, Yelroan. I know you can fight dirty. You just don’t want to. I saw those moves when that money problem occurred—you just need support and a team.”
“I—I appreciate that, but I am not equipped for—”
“Yes, you are. And I saw you hiring yourself out like a common [Streetwalker] from Liscor. You? Disgraceful. That [Princess] doesn’t know what she’s got in you. I was the one who booked you doing all our numbers last week.”
He jumped.
“I wondered who—but Salii, Slave Lady Andra has an entire enterprise. I just have an abacus and a filing cabinet! I cannot take her on.”
This time, the [Secretary] acknowledged the pushback.
“True. You’d need a full staff of people with levels. Heavyweights who can fight on Andra’s terms. And enterprise. Even if Qwera lends you her credentials and plays the spy…”
“Exactly. I don’t have—”
She wrote on a piece of glowing paper, signed and dated it, signed, dated, signed…
After six more signatures, Salii flicked her claws, and Yelroan jumped as something flashed into being in front of him.
“There. Anyone who knows me knows that’s authentic. Take that and go find the Graiz Room in Tower 12, floor…eh, it’s in there. There’s a lovely [Receptionist] working there, a male Drake. Hand it to him and tell him that Salii’s calling for the team. Everyone. It’ll take a bit for them to quit their jobs, but everyone who’s not dead or like Wol and gone to the New Lands will join up. That’s the company I ran.”
Salii’s cutthroat crew of hired guns. As fearsome a crew as that of the Illuminary—just in the business world. She smiled as Yelroan stared at her.
“You’ll have to set up a company in Salazsar. But it’s a good city to use as a base.”
“What—me? I have the inn!”
He protested. But Salii was already busy sending more [Memos].
“You want to stop Andra? That’s where you go. Check in on old Eschowar too. I don’t like him being back. Good luck! Oh, and hire some bodyguards. The [Slavers] love their damn [Assassins]. Actually, I think I have a coupon for the Assassin’s Guild. It’s headquartered in Salazsar.”
She hunted around in her purse, and Yelroan stood there. Suddenly…a Gnoll with a mandate from the Queen of Secretaries. No one else could stop the Slave Lady. Yelroan could try to find someone or—or—
But no one knew. And he was the [Mathematician]. The Gnoll sat there, about to protest that he owed the inn too much. Mrsha too much.
Then he remembered where Arrema, the future version of Mrsha had gone. And he rose…to have a word with Lyonette.
 
——
 
Great changes afoot. The city was changing.
Lism felt it in his bones as he rose. His bed was empty. Normally, there was a great, snoring Gnoll there. And he meant it without any pejoratives, these days.
He’d fallen, annoyingly, head over heels in love. With no less than Krshia Silverfang. If she said jump, he’d have gotten a ladder since his knees weren’t good, but…
And now it was all falling apart. All because they’d risen to the bait, and the Council was lost. Perhaps it’d be a majority of old Councilmembers.
Krshia was upset. He didn’t blame her. Her people felt betrayed—heck, his people felt betrayed, and he’d talked himself hoarse. His outburst yesterday…
He stumbled to the windows and noticed the lack of rain. The rains were stopping. Spring ended. Lism knew it as a Liscorian; the waters would be receding already.
Yet there was still a roaring. A surge like the thrashing of winds, and he wondered if a windstorm was coming in to herald the end of spring. Lism threw open the shutters, bare-chested, squinting out his new glass window to see how bad the weather was.
Maybe it’d make fewer people vote? Dead gods, did he have to count on that? Only when he saw the cloudy skies, with hints of sunlight poking through, did he pause.
There’s no storm. The wind blew no shutters. Yet the roaring continued. He stood there, blinking in the morning, when he realized it was no storm of wind.
It was…voices. Hundreds, nay, thousands of voices. People shouting, stamping in the street. Lism Swifttail stared out the window and realized it was a crowd outside his windows.
His first thought was that the mob had finally come for him, and he was only grateful Krshia wasn’t here. Lism was looking around for the Scroll of Lesser Teleportation they kept for emergencies when they had kept this all secret. He had just found it when he realized they were chanting his name. And not in that angry way.
“Li-sm. Li-sm!”
He poked his head up. Then went to the windows. They were below. Shouting it.
“Li-sm! Li-sm!”
Drakes. Gnolls. Even Humans. Mostly—and this was his demographic, apparently—men. Of varying ages, but ones who liked Lism’s big talk. Young men who wanted to shake up the ways things worked. Old men who’d seen how impossible it was to move beyond the old hierarchies.
People who liked his Carer initiatives—he did have some broadness of his base.
Chanting.
“Li-sm! Li-sm!”
His people. Then Lism saw it. He saw his neighbors standing at their windows, staring at him. People who wanted to protest him, standing in clumps, outnumbered.
Then Lism’s racing heart calmed, and he saw it. He was no Krshia. He wasn’t a Silverfang Gnoll trying to hold together two kinds of Gnoll—and he wasn’t, well, a woman. His impassioned shouting from yesterday?
What kind of red-blooded Liscorian…
The former [Shopkeeper] smiled. He began to reach for a shirt—then he decided against. He strode out in his bathrobe and pants, and the shouting grew louder. He raised his claws in the air and yelled.
“The Lism way! The only way! Lism!”
He leapt down the stairs so hard, he slipped on the last three and went crashing down. The crowd went silent—then Lism popped up. Mud and rainwater on his scales, bruised, but unashamed. He ran through the crowd, slapping hands. Shouting. That was how he burst onto the streets of Liscor.
No apologies, no shame. Only bravado, yelling, and bigger promises.
He felt like he was onto something.

[Reformist Politician Level 25!]
[Skill – Rally the Crowds Obtained!]
[Skill – Don’t You Dare Lie, I Mean It Obtained!]
[Skill – Sense Corruption Obtained!]
[Skill – Tiebreaking Vote (Monthly) Obtained!]
[Skill – If I Keep Talking You’ll See It My Way Obtained!]
 
——
 
Dancing. It wasn’t like Lyonette or Erin had a monopoly on the stuff. Plenty of people had moves. The kind of moves that a stuffy [Princess] and lame [Innkeeper] couldn’t dream of. They just didn’t choose to show it off—
Or danced with any regularity or practice. That was fine because they had lives.
It was just…when someone achieved something like learning a magical dance, and it spread around, it made everyone else think, ‘I could do that’. And wonder what they’d been doing with their time.
Sometimes it was a silly thought, especially if you were someone like the Archmage of Izril. But then you could see Valeterisa and Relc dancing in the common room of the inn for practice. Most of Valeterisa’s thoughts agreed she’d never actually have the time to devote to learning a magical dance, at least not hard ones. But they still thought it was worth doing.
The same for a certain young man with sandy hair who was doing the robot dance moves to an appreciative audience.
“Told you I’ve got moves. See? So, uh, how cool is the magic dance?”
Not-Kevin turned, and few people had actually seen it—the Thronebearers and Xinthe were keeping mum about it, and not even Mrsha the All-Knowing knew. But she was just whirling around with Visma and Ekirra until they fell over.
The little Gnoll cub and lead [Striker] of the Little Crabs had been sad most of the spring. He played in his game, and he was still one of the top players being headhunted by other cities for the little league division of football, but he didn’t run around and put his nose in everything like before.
His parents had been worried because he’d been eating less. But then he’d cheered up recently and put on weight again. No one knew why, and the [Healer] had put it down to childhood, some incident at the playgrounds. Ekirra didn’t talk about it, even to Mrsha or Visma.
He just glanced at the young man dancing and doing Golem-like moves now and then and sniffed the air. And Not-Kevin peeked guiltily at Ekirra as he danced next to Coach Joseph.
Kevin had to be dead, of course. Ekirra had gone to the funeral. The Gnoll didn’t run over and say something because he had a big thought that maybe if [Assassins] killed someone once, they’d do it again. And that, whether anyone noticed it or not, was a Gnoll boy growing a bit older.
There was indeed nothing happy in the inn without sadness waiting in the corner to kick you in the groin and run away.
Even Ishkr was dancing. Trying to do the Highstepper, actually, in the ballroom—he’d bothered and begged someone to help him, and the only person who had anything close to good footwork besides Lyonette, Ilvriss, and the [Knights] was Elia Arcsinger.
She’d given it a go, and so had he—but after two tries and banged shins, they retired to the inn. Ishkr looked disconsolate. Liska was laughing at him, having taken a break to mock her brother. He retorted by asking if she could dance with her new girlfriend at all. Which prompted an existential crisis in Liska—but that was just an amusing affair which occurred in Mirn’s new bar later. Small potatoes.
Ishkr didn’t talk about his thoughts either, most of the time. But he was a bit upset. The dancing didn’t really grab him, but…it was just a reminder. He could see Lyonette and Ilvriss advancing in levels, and he had, for once, slowed. He needed to find his own spark.
“Well, well, look at all the copycats. One simply performs one magical dance and there goes all the mockery.”
Lyonette was gloating in the common room. She pointedly stared at Nanette and Hethon, and the witch flushed, missed her steps, and headbutted Hethon in the chest. There was laughter, merriment, and Wall Lord Ilvriss was sitting at a table, drinking a goat’s milk with his collar parted, no longer weeping. Just…smiling.
An Erin Solstice party, if quieter without Erin. It was not a major victory, but the inn celebrated minor ones because they knew they were important. And, in fact, Ilvriss finally caught one of the guests of the inn who’d been avoiding him.
He was glancing up at the ceiling when he turned.
“Tessa, is that you?”
The Named-rank adventurer wasn’t hiding in the rafters. She was just…sitting in a corner of the inn. She blinked at him.
“Ilvriss, hi.”
“How are you?”
He immediately got up and walked over, and she shrugged.
“Bad. But I’m not dead.”
“You’re still, ah, what’s the word? Detoxing? In withdrawal?”
“Eating shit.”
The Named-ranker moodily stabbed at her plate. Lyonette had bought takeout, another part of Calescent’s time-off strategy. The [Chef] didn’t seem to mind; he was joining the dancers, and he and Bird were dancing.
“Heard you were digging. Got anyone you need dead? I’m not allowed to adventure because I’m ‘suicidal’. Doctor’s orders. I told her that’s normal for adventurers, but she didn’t listen.”
That was all Tessa said. Ilvriss chewed on a response.
“Well, we have a Terland problem. Ulva Terland, and we’ve had [Ranchers] fighting us…”
“Want me to try to take her out?”
“Er…I don’t know if you could.”
“Probably not. I’m bored.”
That was the signal for Lyonette to stride over, plop herself down, and turn to Tessa.
“You didn’t want to dance with me, Tessa, even for practice while Ilvriss was gone! If you’re still bored, we could play a board game or try more activities on our list.”
“Eh.”
The road to recovery was long and, at times, boring, which seemed to be one of the harder battles for the Drake. But Ilvriss was encouraged by the fact that Tessa was actually just sitting there and talking. Even to the other members of staff who came by. In fact, Inkpaper kept coming over to deliver the food.
“I’ve never seen Inkpaper willingly serving food.”
Ilvriss commented, and Lyonette pulled a face.
“He likes Tessa because she asks for something once every six hours at most. She likes him because he leaves her alone. She’s been making book-forts in his library.”
“…You have a library?”
“Of course! Haven’t you seen…? This way!”
The two left to visit the library, and the common room kept being merry without them. Aside from Captain Todi instantly pulling something out. He spread a sheaf of papers down.
“Alright, bets on who thinks those two are gettin’ together?”
He was running a gambling operation, and a lot of the staff and guests crowded around. Menolit had used to do it, but he’d been casual. Todi had made it an illicit operation.
He was also trying to con the odds, so he’d sidled over to bug Menolit, the [Wingman], for his opinion. He also went to pester another expert in love, at least where her mother was concerned.
Mrsha just threw a fry at Todi. She was sitting at a table, sighing over a letter. Eventually, she stopped turning the weather-worn piece of mail over. It had come a long way to reach her, via numerous City Runners, and was beaten up. But then, she’d had [Messages] blocked…she eventually tore open the letter from the Demas Metal tribe and read.
Chieftain Mrell did not know how to write letters to his daughter. Fair enough, she’d almost never responded. So he wrote the letter like a man writing a report.
Blah blah, hoping you’re well, mother is…Mrsha scowled at the writing, then paused when she read something.
 
…We have a wind-powered autohammer finally up and running. It’s loud as can be, has less force than a Level 30 [Blacksmith] with the [Power Hammer] Skill—and it turns off every time the wind isn’t blowing decently hard.
But it’s a start. From what we’ve seen in the place, the real thing will move metal better than what the Dwarves can do, and I’ve seen the ones they have in Dwarfhalls Rest. We’re behind them.
Improving it all is slow. Painfully. We need to get all the materials we don’t have, and we’re bottled by that and technique and magic—everything. But we have time, so far. Chieftain Feshi’s just digging, but Adetr and I are the ones pushing the industry to the next level.
I could maybe send more Demas Metal for this Wall Lord if you were still in need of it. Please let me know…
 
She supposed she had to write him a response. Clever. The Gnoll girl was penning a response when she heard an argument. She glanced up briefly, snorted.
In typical fashion, Ilvriss had gone up to the library and snooted all over it. He and Lyonette were arguing because she’d stocked mostly books from Terandria and northern Izril—no Drake authors. Or Gnollish, which had made the [Princess] embarrassed.
“Mrsha! Can you believe this Drake? He’s doing it again! It’s not ‘politely pointing things out’, it’s classic Salazsarian arrogance, Ilvriss!”
“I didn’t say you had to have more books, and I know you can afford them, Lyonette. You lack for almost any Gnollish authors, so you can’t claim it’s me being a Drake supremacist. Mrsha, back me up—”
The Gnoll girl patently ignored the adults. She eventually waved a quill at her mother.
Why don’t you go and play with Wall Lord Ilvriss somewhere else, Mom? Don’t you have, like, [Cattle Raiders] in shitsville to deal with?
“Mrsha! How dare—are they still attacking you, Ilvriss?”
He hesitated.
“Actually? Less. We have, uh, some decent brawlers, and the Silverfangs are, well, Gnolls. Good at fighting and used to cattle raids. Even besides that, I feel like they’ve lost interest or something. It’s barely two a week right now. But it’s still a huge problem.”
“Darn. Colfa? Can’t you get them to stop?”
“Get [Ranchers] to stop stealing cows. Lyonette, I don’t try to stop you from…beating up innocent chairs? Or Ilvriss from using bidets. You might as well tell a [Thief] to keep his hands out of the till. They don’t stop, and Marvus’ Skill will keep them popping back up. You’re not hurting them.”
The adults were talking around Mrsha’s table as she furiously wrote to her father about the annoyances surrounding her. However, Mrsha was listening, and her ears perked up as Lyonette hmmed.
“It reminds me of Kaliv, honestly. Or Gaiil-Drome.”
“Your allies? Don’t you all get along in the Dawn Concordat?”
“Please, Ilvriss. You know allies.”
“Ah. Quite. I suppose recent events have kept you all friendlier. How bad is it in peace?”
“Griffins, wild or tamed, sniping Calanfer’s own herds—Kaliv always wants more food. And Gaiil-Drome are half-Elves.”
Colfa propped her hands on her hips, amused.
“Not a lot of meat-eaters around there, I’d wager. Even if I know Ceria’s different, don’t half-Elves eschew meat culturally?”
“They do, so they ‘liberate’ plants or animals. It’s actually worse than Kaliv. Before you laugh—imagine a half-Elf wanders around your town, city, hamlet, and sees some magnificent pumpkin that’s too good for you to just eat. It’d make seedlings and grow so beautifully, so they just…take it. Or they find this darling cat, and since you’re a damned Human, they walk off with it.”
“What, they steal pets?”
“We’ve had decades-long border conflicts over it. Ugh. That’s what a Thronebearer is good for.”
“Well, how does Calanfer deal with them?”
“Mostly, we negotiate, have level-headed people on the other kingdom’s sides sort it out. But if we need to, we’re not above being petty. One time, we had eight Thronebearers infiltrate Gaiil-Drome’s palace and steal the [Queen]’s favorite Sariant Lamb! She was hosted at the Eternal Throne and liked it so much that after eight months, she didn’t want to go home. That’s how you poke them back. Say…”
Lyonette went still, then she tapped her lips thoughtfully.
“…Poke them back. Ilvriss, you’ve never so much as fired an arrow the [Ranchers]’ way, have you? No wonder they’re so emboldened.”
“If you’re suggesting I ride out and beat them up on the job, Lyonette, that sounds like a good way to start an all-out fight. And they’re just itching for an excuse to escalate, some of the hotheads. We can’t survive a [Rancher] war, even if I do have actual [Soldiers].”
Ilvriss was cautious, but Mrsha’s head rose as she cottoned onto her mother’s line of thought. She held up a card.
Yo, Mom. Are you pondering what I’m pondering?
The Gnoll girl had the card handed back to her.
“Mrsha, I told you, your language is slipping! Don’t ‘yo’ me.”
The card was amended and handed back.
I don’t wanna hear that from you. I’ve heard you swear. Don’t make me tell Ilvriss your idea and pretend I came up with it.
Her daughter was getting so…Lyonette scowled, but hurried to explain.
“Ilvriss, Colfa. Hear me out, and if it’s a bad idea, we’ll say no more of it. But what if we gave the Lischelles a taste of their own medicine?”
The [Princess] leaned forwards. Colfa’s eyes lit up, and she gave Lyonette a wickedly sharp grin. Ilvriss frowned.
“That sounds exactly like one of Dramm’s stupid ideas, Lyonette.”
She shoved one shoulder.
“Don’t be rude, Ilvriss! I’m not done, and we’ll do it far more cleverly.”
“You’re right, I apologize, that was way over the line. Dead gods, I am snarky.”
The adults conferred, then ran off like excited children to make plans. Mrsha sighed as Visma and Ekirra joined her at the table. Visma fanned herself as she tugged at her dress, and Ekirra put down some paperwork on the table. Homework for school.
“The adults being silly again?”
Yup. It’s good for them, so I indulge Mother.
Visma nodded seriously.
“I should tell my mother to take a break more.”
They turned to Ekirra to say something profoundly old and questioning the maturity of their alleged seniors. He screwed his face up.
“I, uh, I gotta file my taxes! Darn taxes, taking all my money.”
The two girls sighed and nodded wisely.
Taxes.
 
——
 
Ilvriss was always refreshed when he came back from Liscor. It was a noticeable thing, such that everyone in the expedition had begun talking about ‘going to Liscor’ when referring to any time off.
There had been envy, of course, for the Wall Lord getting to leave the cowpat crusted lands—and lest one think this was an exaggeration, no. Given the amount of bovines and other grazers, someone had once calculated that the Cowpat Plains’ topsoil was probably at least 1/8th manure in some stage of decomposition.
However, recent events had changed things. Vacation days were still a luxury, but they weren’t being fought over as hard. Amenities had come to the campsite.
Working toilets, pumped water from wells, cooling spells and recreation thanks to Lady Damia, and the undead attacks and [Rancher] brawls had slowed. In fact…one look at someone like Foreman Rolx walking around with his new pickaxe or seeing him at work, shoulder-to-shoulder with the rest of the [Miners] now trying to even keep up with him, and you felt it.
Opportunity. Not for just the Gemscale Company, but maybe for the ordinary grunts like you.
It wasn’t all positives, of course. Some people didn’t like the brothel or that Drake who ran it. And there was some kind of kerfuffle about one of the Advance Miners or something. He’d been cursed or…but you got told to knock it off if you wanted to gossip.
It wasn’t so much ‘don’t ask, don’t tell’ as much as ‘stop hassling me about it’. Not perfect. And Georgie thought it wouldn’t last, but he had to admit, after two days back on the job, he’d stopped watching his backtail.
Wall Lord Ilvriss had been as good as his word, again, for now. He’d just put Georgie back to work as an Advance Miner, but instead of his old outfit, Georgie found himself working with none other than Rolx’s dig squad. And a number of other Advance Miners and officials who he knew.
None of them brought up the rumors. Adjutant Welsca addressed the new work shift in a one minute announcement at muster because time was money.
“We are here to dig, Drakes, Gnolls, and, uh, Humans. Bring up any issues to me of actual, substantive merit, but I want all your attention on the digging. Those undead aren’t the only potential threats down there. On a side note, be aware that the work day will end two hours early today, and there will be an announcement from Wall Lord Ilvriss and a request for volunteers.”
Then they got to it. Silverfangs were working as Dig Team Kippish next to Rolx, and both groups just…mined. Mining Leader Maiss introduced Georgie around, and the Advance Miner was actually most worried because, uh, he’d broken Maiss’ jaw.
The big Drake with a rather protruding jawline now didn’t appear exactly friendly, but he just slapped Georgie on the shoulder, hard, and grinned.
“You get to buy the first round after work, Miner Georgie, and if I catch you brawling again, it won’t be on my time. Understood? No more words on the jaw or anything else. I should have ducked. Too used to a helmet.”
Georgie nodded.
“Er, right, sorry, Mining Leader. Anything I should know about Rolx?”
Maiss paused and scratched his scales under his chin.
“Well, we’re slow. We’ve picked up the business, but we’re all greenhorns. You just lead, and we’ll try to keep up. We might have to slow you down to catch up, but that’s it.”
“Got it.”
 
——
 
Rolx wasn’t actually that incompetent. They just didn’t have Skills, a point Georgie noticed. And they weren’t adept—three times, he had to point out how they were slowing work by not organizing a line to haul away soil and rocks, and they didn’t have a sorting bin…Maiss heard him out, gave orders, and they fixed it.
It was a big team of eighty Drakes. The ones up front worked hard, sweating and cursing, but they rotated out twice as fast. What did they do? Well, rest—then hit one of the gymnasiums that Georgie had never wanted to go to. Lift weights, spar on practice mats—
No [Miner] had the energy for that kind of thing after work. But Maiss just offered Georgie a chance to rest, then made zero comment about it. They did end up talking, though; you had to talk—or have a scrying orb playing the news or music—or go crazy.
“I get that the other [Miner] teams hate our guts. We’re just loyalty hires. Tell the others that if they’re upset.”
“Really? You’ve been with the Gemscale company that long?”
Georgie was leading the way with his enchanted pickaxe; he reckoned they’d be almost up to par with his pickaxe alone. It was insane. Maiss was shovelling up the weird bricks of converted stone into wheelbarrows to be rolled up the shaft. They worked in forty-minute bursts. The pickaxe did stop functioning after about that long, but it recharged insanely fast, especially if you dropped it next to some magical gems; Salazsar had plenty even if they weren’t mining for gems here. The Mining Leader nodded as he leaned on his shovel.
“I’ve been with Wall Lord Ilvriss for…eleven years now? Different roles.”
“Huh. You like it?”
“Ups and downs. Can’t complain. You hear about other companies and how you get treated if you’re hurt. Still, sometimes, I wondered if I’d ever make pension. It’s thirty years to even get to one—”
“Yeah. You’re not making that in Advance Miner work.”
“Don’t you get triple the rate? If you’d been here ten years as an Advance Miner, you’d already be there.”
“Really? Damn. I never thought I’d be a full-time company man.”
Maiss glanced at Georgie as the Drake wiped at the sweat streaming from under his helmet. Just for one second Georgie’s scales tingled, and he checked his clothing—but Maiss only shrugged.
“I’ll be honest, before last year, I was thinking maybe I’d join another outfit in time. Or go into private business, even. Not that I’m getting any younger. But that’s what you have to do, to really make your mark. Hard to retire on regular pay and live large.”
Georgie nodded. He’d heard the same thing a lot of times. Just empty dreams, but…he was glad to talk about this kind of thing, because he felt the same way but seldom got to discuss it.
“It’s always owning your own shit. Same thing with [Miners] but collecting permits, having representatives, and getting paperwork through—can’t do it unless you have a lot of money, and the big companies can’t be beat.”
“Exactly. It’s easier with soldiering and other professions, or so I’ve heard. Nevermind that. Like I said, until recently I felt like I needed to go independent. But with this dig and that—”
He nodded at Georgie’s pickaxe, and the Advance Miner nodded too.
“It’s a three year contract to keep it.”
“Only three years? Same with the Silverfangs, really. Gnolls in the north is strange. I can’t think they want to live here, but the fact we’ve made it months without being killed in our sleep is odd.”
They were working close enough to Dig Team Kippish as they hauled more materials back up the shaft that some of the Gnolls heard, even forty feet away. One of the Silverfangs, a Gnoll with a silver piercing in one ear, leaned on his tools and grinned.
The Silverfang Gnolls were interesting. They actually had different tools than the Salazsarians, and a different style. Unlike Georgie, who swung a pickaxe or shovel in a classic position, the Silverfang Gnolls were right up against whatever they were digging and had claw-blades.
Like knuckle dusters, but sturdier, an entire gauntlet they used to scoop dirt out. It left them often buried in soil or looser particulates, but they could move it very quickly. The [Miners] claimed they could also hear the strata moving around them, and it avoided monsters or collapses most of the time.
Georgie still thought Salazsar was far better at actually digging through stone with their systems, but Team Kippish was working with a will. However, the Gnoll just shouted.
“If we’re staying in the north, my vote won’t be for here! This place stinks almost half as bad as you two!”
He had nose plugs in, even in the mines. Georgie and Maiss shouted insults back, then Georgie waved a claw around.
“It’s not that bad down here, is it?”
The Silverfangs just eyed him. The Gnoll [Miner], whose name was Vitteh, shook his head.
“It’s almost worse down here. Our poor cubs had to go back with some of the tribe because they couldn’t stand the smell. Mind you, we like the food and open landscape. Just—gah! What do they feed those cows? Also—I keep finding gemstones. Is that normal?”
“Gemscale Company Skill. Just declare them, and unless it’s a magical gemstone, they’re ordinary profits. How many did you get?”
“Uh, eleven today? Small ones, but—”
Georgie nearly slipped as Maiss’ brows rose.
“How? It’s supposed to be rare, even with the Skill.”
“Well, I’m a [Lucky Miner].”
Vitteh winked. Georgie and Maiss strolled over and asked about it, and Vitteh confirmed that he was no Doombearer—not that the Silverfangs were anything less than allies—but he’d earned it after the Meeting of Tribes. Maiss grunted.
“I was there. With Wall Lord Ilvriss, obviously. You all fancy cards or something after this? There’s some games in Thirthoof, actually. That brothel has a bunch set up.”
“The brothel does?”
“Apparently. Some new game from Liscor where you have a bunch of balls and sticks and hit them around—those Sisters of Chell are obsessed with it.”
“As long as I’m not gambling away my paycheck, sure. Miner Georgie and Maiss, yes? Let me introduce you to our Ekhtouch [Miner]. He’s the one who punched out that [Cattle Baron].”
“Ah, I owe him a drink—”
They chatted for twenty minutes and agreed to go out on the town until Welsca found them jawing and sent them back to work. And Georgie found himself chewing on his cigar and waiting for the catch. Because this felt…
Decent. Too damn decent. He was glad when he was reminded the work day ended two hours early, and he found Wall Lord Ilvriss addressing all the dig teams and asking for volunteers. Ilvriss caught his eye just once, and Georgie smiled grimly.
There had to be a catch. And the catch was—he raised his clawed hand.
You want my loyalty? You got it. He lined up with a number of eager Drakes and Gnolls—Maiss was apologizing to the others as well for missing the drinks because he was volunteering—when Ilvriss pointed.
“No Advance Miners or team leaders. Out of line, you two!”
…He kicked Georgie and Maiss out of the volunteer crew. They stood to one side. Georgie stared at Ilvriss. He didn’t want them? Then he saw a [Princess] being followed around by Lady Damia like a puppy following an older dog, and he realized something was up.
He stood there, confused, until Maiss slapped him on the shoulder.
“Alright, let’s get cleaned up. Damnit, the one time something fun happens…”
They walked off, and Georgie kept waiting for the catch. Which might have been getting fleeced by the damn Sisters of Chell, along with Maiss and everyone else, out of several silver coins. But if that was all—
Georgie would take it.
 
——
 
It was approaching dusk as the sun cast long rays over the tents, and she was several days late when Lady Desinee El finally found Wall Lord Ilvriss’ camp.
In fairness, she had meant it when she agreed to ride out to show him the House of El’s merchandise. But she’d had to wait in Invrisil for a restock from the family and then find her way all the way to the delightfully-named ‘Cowpat Plains’.
There weren’t exactly that many Overnight Carriages who wanted to make the journey out, and Lady Desinee had eventually had to just ride the entire darn way from the magical door. And—this was important—
No one had a map to Wall Lord Ilvriss’ camp. She’d had to ask around Thirthoof and been told ‘go that way and you’ll find it’.
Then she had to reach the camp and talk her way past the security. After half an hour of security checks, she was issued a temporary pass and told the Wall Lord was at ‘Dig Site B’. Desinee was trying to be polite, but she was tired and rather wished she’d booked accommodations.
Maybe I can beg a tent rather than having to ride back to town and do this in the morning. She sighed as she rehearsed her presentation. He might not even buy anything. This was why she didn’t actually want to do sales. She still had nightmares, though. They’d gotten worse since coming to Liscor. That had been a mistake, since she’d seen the Bone Giant outside the city, still trapped by the ribbons. The Watch had built up a lot of stone around its limbs in case it moved, but she had the image of it just ripping the stones apart and beginning to attack—
Lady Desinee El didn’t find the Wall Lord at Dig Site B. [Miners] were marching off from work, and one of them wolf-whistled as she rode past them. He got a nudge and shut up—she sighed.
“Wall Lord Ilvriss is where?”
“I think he was riding northwards, Miss, er, Lady. Not sure why. You might catch him if you’re fast.”
A Wall Lord Dramm pointed her northwards, and she thought she just saw a bunch of dots on the horizon. Desinee groaned.
Turn around or…? She kicked her horse in the side and sped forwards. She was one woman on horseback, and she did know how to ride; she was Maviola El’s niece. Plus, the Drake had mentioned that Ilvriss had company. Lyonette du Marquin and a [Lady] of House Reinhart?
“I hope the Reinharts aren’t causing trouble here. If I don’t find out what they’re up to, I won’t hear the end of it from Deilan. Giddy up!”
Her mare gave Desinee a reproachful look, but sped up. The [Lady] was riding light with just a bag of holding, so she was confident she’d catch the Wall Lord and his far-larger company. It was only…she hadn’t been riding nearly as much until recently.
Her butt hurt. Desinee let her mare accelerate into a full canter, then realized the Wall Lord was moving fast! She let the mare run harder, and the excited horse cut loose on the wide, flat plains. Desinee said—
“Ow, ow, ow. My family for a healing potion!”
If she just had one, she’d be able to drink it and not worry about being saddle-sore! But on the horse galloped, and Desinee did everything to avoid aggravating the posterior pain.
First, she stood up in the stirrups. But her legs got tired, so after five minutes of that, she leaned on the pommel, but that was uncomfortable. Then she tried side-saddle, but that was so unpleasant at a near-gallop that—
“Are you insane?”
She was nearly on the Drakes when a rider rode past her, shouting. Desinee had to grab for her horse’s saddle as she started, and a white-haired woman appeared next to Desinee, horrified.
“What are you doing? Riding side-saddle at a gallop? You’ll break your neck, you fool!”
Desinee swung herself back across the horse’s back, flushing, and called over.
“I’m quite fine, Miss! Is this Wall Lord Ilvriss’ company?”
This was a Human woman with white hair and a riding dress suited to farmwork. Desinee feared she’d gone the wrong way, but the woman simply shouted back.
“It is! I am Colfa val Lischelle-Drakle—who are you? Why are you here?”
She eyed Desinee as skeptically as Desinee eyed the woman. Lischelle-Drakle? Wasn’t that one of the local [Rancher] clans…? Desinee had been told to present the House of El’s wares to them while she was here, just in case.
“I am Lady Desinee of the House of El! Greetings! I’m here to present some items for Wall Lord Ilvriss?”
“The House of…? This isn’t a good time for it.”
“Well, I have ridden a very long ways, Miss val Lischelle, and I’d just like to ask the Wall Lord’s permission to stay at his camp until his earliest convenience. Is the Wall Lord here?”
Desinee stood up in the stirrups again, and Colfa once more gave her a horrified look. But Desinee saw Ilvriss and urged her mare forwards—he was riding fast through the advancing night.
“Hold on, this isn’t—how are you doing that?”
“Doing what?”
“Riding that horse! You’re a [Lady] of the House of El, aren’t you?”
Desinee gave Colfa a blank glance. The Vampiress was a country-raised girl herself, and she might not have been used to riding recently, but you knew what they said about riding horses…it was as easy to remember as riding a horse.
However, even Colfa would have thought twice about some of the ways Desinee was moving about her horse at the speed it was going. For the rather stereotypical [Lady] in a riding dress? It boggled Colfa’s mind just long enough for Desinee to move past her. And then the Vampiress was shouting.
“Turn back! This isn’t—”
Too late. Lady Desinee was almost upon the Wall Lord as he slowed, and she saw a red-haired young woman riding next to him with a golden [Knight]. And, for some reason, a familiar young woman with an eager smile.
I met her. What did I know about Lady Damia, again? Isn’t she the one who sleeps with her brother? Desinee was utterly confused—then she realized she was surrounded by Drakes, Gnolls, a few Humans, all of whom had cloth handkerchiefs tied around their noses. Most were grinning. Wall Lord Ilvriss raised a claw and called out.
“Here we go! Remember, in and out—no games, no brawling. If you’re in danger, get clear! Fighters? With me! Lady Damia, don’t break your neck.”
“Yes, Wall Lord!”
“Er, hello, excuse me, Wall Lord Ilvriss? Wall Lord, it’s me, Lady Desinee—”
The [Lady] was trying to get to the front of whatever this was, now embarrassed, worried she was interrupting something important. She was riding through the Drakes as she saw they’d come to a pasture of some kind.
Just some fenced-off land. A big fence for a vast herd of…cattle? She peered at the giant bovines with the disinterest of someone who didn’t want to know where her food came from. Then she saw some Drakes get off their horses and pull the pieces of wood up and remove part of the fence.
Odd. There was a gate over yonder. And then a Gnoll with tribal markings and feathers tied to her saddles was riding forwards with a group of eight. She whooped, then howled, a loud, excited sound, as Desinee slowed.
Huntress Rayna’s howl made the sleepy cows start. They turned, some getting to their feet, and saw the Gnolls riding at them from the left. Nervously, they clustered together as Rayna rode around them in a slow circle. Then she began to ride at them from the rear and shouted.
“Get moving! Raah, rah!”
She was herding the cows towards the break in the fence. All she had to do was get one cow in front moving and the rest followed—when they tried to veer left, one of the Silverfang Gnolls rode out and corralled them back into position, forcing their momentum.
Everyone moved aside as the first worried bovine charged out the fence. Desinee El blinked.
My, that was fast. Wouldn’t they risk some of the cows getting hurt at that speed? But she didn’t know ranching. They were being driven back the way they’d come, and she turned and saw the group following Ilvriss breaking apart, repeating the process. Some were riding for another distant herd, and she wondered why the Wall Lord didn’t just have the people minding this herd of cows do this.
Because surely there were some; one did not leave this much prime cattle alone. Sure enough, there was a pair of Humans who rode towards the commotion. A young man and woman, on horseback, who stopped and stared as they saw Rayna racing away—and a bunch of heads of cattle. They turned, and the Wall Lord pointed.
“Ah, there’s our company. Hello! Don’t mind us! Lyonette, I think that’s the main herd and bull enclosure. With me.”
He began riding forwards as Colfa joined Lyonette and Damia Reinhart split off. Desinee tried to go forwards again, and then she saw the two young Humans break out of their stupor. One turned and began racing backwards. The other cupped her hands to her mouth and shouted.
“—Cattle raid! Dead gods—cattle raid! Them Drakes are stealing our herds! Get up! Get up—”
It took a second for Desinee to put two and two together. Then she heard another whoop and more shouting, and Ilvriss began galloping forwards. Her mouth fell open in horror.
He was stealing cows? She gaped right and left, then realized where she was.
Not his camp, but Lischelle’s farmstead. And the Wall Lord was, at this moment, trying to steal every single cow around…
Then it was chaos as [Ranchers] poured out, shouting, and Drakes and Gnolls and Humans leapt down and began punching them while the others tried to make off with the cows. They weren’t the best at it, but they knew how it was done because it had happened so many times to them. The Silverfangs did know how to move the frightened bovines.
“Oh no. Uh oh. I can’t be part of—”
Desinee was keenly aware how well this would be received. She cast around for somewhere to hide as Ilvriss rode forwards.
Laughing. And there was a [Princess] with him!
 
——
 
Lyonette du Marquin had overridden all of Ushar’s objections to be part of this. She hadn’t taken Nanette or Mrsha; it was dangerous after all, and Rhisveri would just shout at her, the annoying Wyrm.
But she had her heart in her throat as Ilvriss cut one fence apart with his sword and hauled the pieces of wood away. She saw a bunch of [Ranchers] running at them.
“Stop! You bastards—”
“Time for revenge!”
Captain Osthia drop-kicked the lead [Rancher], and her Sisters of Chell jumped the [Ranchers]. Ilvriss grinned.
“Rubirel Guard? Hold ‘em off, and if any of you see Marvus, let me know! I hope he’s not here.”
They’d hit one of the main ranches of the Lischelles. Colfa knew where they were, and she was whistling in a curious way. The cows in this pasture peered up, and several got up and began trotting her way.
“I knew they kept the old whistles. Hard to teach cows new ones. Here they come…get moving, you silly things! This way, nice and easy!”
The [Ranchers] were brawling with Ilvriss’ people, and Lyonette was not going to join a fistfight in her condition, but she added to Colfa’s voice, laughing with delight. Someone saw Colfa whistling the cows out and shouted.
“You damn traitor—”
She turned as a [Ranchhand] charged her, and Colfa punched the other woman in the chest, then followed up with a kick. She dusted her hands.
“Reminds me of being young and doing this to the Golvas. Aronia and Marvus won’t be able to live this one down. Getting stolen from by Drakes! The humiliation!”
The funny thing was that they’d never have expected it. Not from Ilvriss. He’d been so defensive that when he finally got them—Lyonette turned her head.
“Is Lady Damia riding a cow?”
The [Lady] was riding off at the head of another herd, and Lyonette shook her head. What a strange Reinhart. Mrsha hadn’t been lying. Ilvriss emerged from the fighting, panting.
“Okay, I’m done. Let’s get this herd and go. Someone punched me in the stomach, and I remembered why I’m too old for this.”
“You’re no fun.”
Lyonette teased him as he glowered.
“You try fighting these [Ranchers]. Ancestors, I’m just glad they’re not firing arrows.”
Colfa had been laughing, but she stopped warily.
“They don’t have to. Bull’s coming. Lyonette, get back.”
There was a reason no one stole from the Lischelles. Ushar gulped as there was a braying moo, and Ilvriss lost his smile.
The bulls that Marvus raised were intelligent, huge, and like all bulls, protective of their herds. They killed everything, including the Drake boy who’d been foolish enough to jump the fence.
This one came out of the pasture fast, horns down, and Lyonette swallowed when she saw it coming.
Eternal Throne, she’d seen bulls before, but always at a distance! This one was terrifying, but Ilvriss just raised his claw.
“Right. Ready?”
“Yessir. Ready!”
Welsca responded instantly, and reached for her side as she looked around. Ilvriss had kept his bodyguards around him as well as a handful of Drakes who hadn’t joined the fighting. They shifted, and Lyonette heard clicks around her. This had been Ilvriss’ addition to her plan. He waited, claw raised.
The bull was maybe a hundred feet distant and closing faster than Lyonette thought a creature its size could move when Ilvriss closed his fist.
“Now. Loose!”
The crack was every Drake around Lyonette discharging the crossbows they’d drawn. There was also the thft of arrows being loosed from bows. The angry braying faltered, and Lyonette saw the charging bull stumble, slow—
Then keep coming. 
“Ancestors, it really could kill most monsters.”
Ilvriss murmured. He raised his hand again.
“Headshots. [Glitter Volley] on me. One, two, three—loose.”
The second volley took the bull down. It slid as it collapsed, and one of the [Ranchers] cried out.
“They just shot Redman! They just—”
“Get those cattle out of there. Someone make sure it’s dead—don’t get near it.”
Ilvriss calmly turned his horse. He locked eyes with the horrified [Ranchers] and nodded to them.
“Apologies for that. But I’m bad at rustling cows, and I don’t intend to lose anyone. Welsca, drop any other bulls guarding their herds. Let’s get out of here before they actually come after us.”
Then everyone was riding. Riding, and Lyonette turned and felt a pang as she saw the motionless bull lying there, but Colfa began shouting, urging the cows onwards, and the Sisters and Osthia caught up. Then the people around her were cheering.
By the time they got back to camp with the cows to herd them into pastures, everyone had turned out to cheer. Ilvriss jumped down from his horse, grinning despite himself, as Dramm rushed over.
“Wall Lord! I just heard—you stole their cows?”
Like Liuss? But Ilvriss corrected the Drake.
“We reminded them two can play their game, Dramm. No casualties—aside from their prime bulls—and I think we’ve recovered some of all the gold they’ve taken from us. No more raids, not unless they keep coming back. Understood?”
That’s why he hadn’t taken Dramm, but the younger Wall Lord stood there.
“You shot their bull?”
“Two, I think. I wasn’t about to take chances after poor Liuss. Come on, I have a [Message] to send. Miss Lyonette? You in one piece or is Mrsha going to kill me?”
“Right here, Wall Lord.”
Lyonette was smiling as she got down, wincing from all the hard riding, but her daughters would be green with envy about missing this! She was all ready to celebrate when Colfa peered around.
“Where’s that [Lady] of El that was with us? Did you see her? I tried to tell her what we were doing, but she was confused…”
Ilvriss stopped smiling.
“Lady of El? What Lady…do you mean Desinee El? Why was she with us?”
He turned to Lyonette, who hadn’t seen her, and Dramm paled. In his defense, he hadn’t been told where Ilvriss was going. Unfortunately, despite everyone else having made it back, there was no Desinee El among the riders.
 
——
 
Marvus Lischelle couldn’t believe his eyes when he came riding out as fast as he could to find the aftermath of the cattle raid. He stood there, sleep-deprived, stressed—but it wasn’t a charnel pit.
“It wasn’t the half-Elf? It was that Drake?”
His voice cracked as a man with two black eyes stood in front of him.
“No sir, Marvus. It was just the Wall Lord. And them damn Gnolls and your wife’s sister! They grabbed all the cattle and—well—two of our bulls. Shot ‘em dead.”
Redman, one of the prime bulls, was lying in his field, feathered with arrows and crossbow bolts. Marvus felt a pang. He walked over, and his fists clenched as he stared at the lolling tongue and empty eyes of the bull. Flies were already buzzing, so he snapped.
“What’re you lot doing? Get him butchered and trussed up—don’t waste Redman! He’s still good unless the arrows were poisoned. Check! How many did we lose? Those shit-eating, cow-buggering—”
He started swearing, going red with fury as his people shook off the shock. Furious and embarrassed. He’d not thought they could do that to him. You almost had to admire that Drake’s balls, if he had them. Marvus didn’t want to know about that kind of thing.
Aside from the bulls, it was a huge cattle raid. But you didn’t kill bulls. That was just—he vowed vengeance, then wondered if the Wall Lord would do it again.
If I have to watch all our ranches where he’s bordering us, I’ll not have the men to guard our other pastures and deal with that half-Elf. And the Golvas and Billgerhorns aren’t going to pass up the opportunity, even now. Unless I call a truce—
But the bulls. Marvus turned his head to the dead bulls, and then one of his people rode up.
“Marvus? I don’t want to bother you, but we just got a message. That Drake. It ain’t addressed to you specific, but there’s no other way to read it. Aronia wants t’know how bad it is.”
“Give it here.”
If it was a taunt, Marvus would take it on the chin and come at the Wall Lord, maybe even that night with—he read the [Message], blinked, and read again.
It wasn’t addressed to him, indeed. It wasn’t even a classic [Message], but something you got through the Merchant’s Guild. There was no ‘Rancher’s Guild’ because that was stupid, so Marvus simply had a Merchant’s Guild account, and they sent him correspondence like this all the time.
Requests for heads of cattle. You could bid on them if they were open, or get in contact to satisfy orders. In this case, it was just an open request from Wall Lord Ilvriss.
Wanted. Prime bull beef. Bull’s tongues. Bull’s testicles—with a request to please separate those and mark them as alchemical goods, which was standard—he was asking for a lot.
Literal tons. About as much as…you’d get from two prime Lischelle bulls, though the Drake had rounded down. And the price?
Marvus blinked, then read it again. He did a bit of quick math and put the cost at roughly twice the going rate for Lischelle beef, on the gold coin. The Drake was overpaying for…
The [Cattle Baron]’s head slowly rose, and he took another glance at the two dead bulls. Then it turned towards the direction of the Wall Lord’s camp.
“Marvus?”
“So that’s his play. Is that Drake hospitality or something? Damnit.”
“You want us to ignore it?”
Marvus chewed on his lip.
“You mad? We don’t have a buyer, and they’re dead. Make sure there’s no problems, then fill the order. I’ll talk to Aronia.”
He crumpled the paper up and tossed it aside. Redman had been a good bull, damnit. But he was a mankiller like all of them. Unless you had a Skill, he would murder any idiot Drakes trying to take his herd, just like that poor boy.
“Hm. Hmpf. Damnit. How the hell am I…? Did Aronia have to chase Colfa off without so much as a sit-down to talk and—? Can’t let it slide. It’s halfway classy, paying for it. Halfway!”
He snapped at a young woman who gave him a wide-eyed gaze. Marvus stomped around, swearing. Thinking.
“Dad? You aren’t planning on going off and fighting the Drake, were you? Ma sent me to check on you. You’re too mad, she says. Might get bad.”
Burlise had ridden over. Marvus turned to his eldest daughter and snapped.
“I’m not going after him right off. I gotta think.”
“Phew. I was worried I’d have to lasso you and have everyone hold you down. I heard they killed Redman and took nearly three hundred head of cattle.”
She took off her hat and wiped her forehead. Marvus’ face grew even more sour.
“They did. Your aunt helped ‘em. I have to think. Damn, damn…alright.”
He took off his hat, tossed it down, but didn’t step on it since he’d never get the manure off. Marvus spat.
“Fine! What the hell is he digging up?”
Burlise cocked her head at him. Marvus finally asked the question as he stuck his thumbs in his belt. Once they’d cleaned this up, he’d have to have a long chat with Aronia. She was madder than he was, so he was dreading it, and maybe he’d ride out to have a word with Taldo Rominet, who had good eyes.
Marvus was thinking, grinding his teeth together, when he heard the scream. High, female, and followed by the sound that made his blood run cold each time he heard it. The loudest braying roar a bull could make—because it came from the loudest one. The biggest.
“That’s Big Burlap. Did someone get in his pen?”
Burlise took off as Marvus whistled for his horse. He heard her yelling and more shouts.
“Dad! Daaaad—someone’s in his pasture!”
Oh dead gods. The horror that had been the last cookout hit Marvus again, and he was running, forgetting his horse. One of the Wall Lord’s people or his?
“Skills! Wands! Stop—”
Too late. He knew it was too late. If you jumped that fence, or ducked under it, as a certain [Lady] might have done to avoid being caught up in the cattle raid, you might live.
If you were lucky, Big Burlap wouldn’t smell or see you; it was a big pasture. But he had a good nose, and he had those eyes which could roll around and see almost in every direction. He knew his herd. His mates, his children—and then he’d find you, sneak up on you, and when you ran—
Lady Desinee El had been sneaking around the pasture, possibly thinking to ride off, but the bull had spotted her and caught her. She made it all of fifty paces towards the fence where [Ranchers] were screaming at her to run, trying to throw Skills or tag the bull with wands to slow or tranquilize him.
She never made it. Big Burlap got to her a full minute before Marvus reached the pasture fence, clutching at his chest, wheezing for air and saw—
“Help me! Oh, Five Families—heeeeeeeeeeeeeelp!”
He did not see a dead woman trampled into a pile of gore. Marvus halted, slack jawed, and instead saw a terrified [Lady] on top of Big Burlap as the bull kicked and leapt, all four hooves clearing the ground as he kicked up and back.
He was trying to throw Lady Desinee El off, but despite the motion, which would have sent most [Riders] flying—the [Lady] stayed on his back. And she was screaming.
“Someone stop him! Stop standing there or I’ll die!”
All the [Ranchers] were just gazing up at her, dumbstruck. Marvus strode over, grabbed a wand, and began shooting [Rays of Weakening] into Big Burlap, heedless of the cost.
“Burlise, lasso him but don’t hold onto the rope or he’ll yank your arms out of their sockets! Get him calmed down before he tosses her!”
The angry bull took two dozen Tier 2 spells without even slowing. His eyes were red, and he was roaring, shaking his head—he even collapsed and tried to roll Desinee El over, but she just stayed on top of him.
Neither Marvus nor Big Burlap could believe it. As the spells finally began to work, the bull stood upright, all five thousand pounds of him, and actually turned his head to stare at the [Lady] sitting astride his back.
How the hell are you doing that? He did one more flying kick, planting his hooves and kicking back and up and sending his body arching diagonally in the air with an explosive movement of such force that the dirt clods he kicked up knocked a man standing behind the fence flat.
And Lady Desinee El pushed herself up with him, mitigating the kick and rippling of his muscles, caught herself at the apex of his kick, and fell with him. She winced as she landed, but stayed on top.
“Is he—is he calming down? Can I get off?”
Big Burlap began to lie down. Almost—Marvus thought—succumbing to the sleep spells out of embarrassment. Pretending that had been what stopped him from murdering her. Even when his head was down, Marvus kept shouting.
“Don’t get off him! Wait ‘till we’ve got him roped up! Then you can go off! He might get up and gore you!”
Desinee hollered back.
“No need to tell me twice! My great aunt told me never to get off a bull once I was on it until I knew it was fully asleep or I had something to stand behind!”
“Who told you to get on a bull’s back?”
Marvus was astounded when she finally leapt off and ran for the fence. Desinee El tried to climb it, then swung herself under the fence’s slats. She was fully winded, red-faced, pale from fear—and alive. She stood as the [Ranchers] gathered around the only person to ever survive a round with Big Burlap, in awe.
If Nerul was unto the nascent gymnasium mythology, Lady Desinee El had become part of ranching lore. She panted for air as she introduced herself.
“I am very sorry about—I wasn’t part of Wall Lord Ilvriss’ company! I am Lady Desinee El, presenting the House of El’s creations, and I had no idea what this was about! I was staying back when I entered the pasture to keep out of the way and—”
“The House of El?”
Everyone drew back in disbelief. The most important noble to visit the Cowpat Plains in over, what, fifteen years? She was nothing like what Marvus expected, and he realized they had nearly gotten a scion of the House of El killed, a moment of true horror. But Desinee reassured him.
“Merely one of the members of the House of El. That bull is dangerous!”
“Yes, he’s killed cattle thieves before—I’ve never seen anyone stay on his back, and that’s the only place you might be safe. One man tried it when he was still growing—he bucked him and broke every rib he had. How did you stay on?”
Desinee was patting at her forehead, but she managed a smile.
“Well, I am Lady Maviola El’s niece. Lady Firestarter? She taught everyone in the family how to ride as children. A Skill, in fact.”
The late [Lady] of the House of El? Marvus had seen her on the scrying orb, though he knew her as—
“One of the most famous horse thieves of Izril? That [Lady]?”
“She said she had the record for most bull rides in her lifetime. And that she’d seen eight peers break their necks trying to copy her. We’re not like Tyrion Veltras, but the House of El are the best riders among the Five Families. Not that it’s saying much. Terlands will only ride their silly Golem Horses, Wellfar ride ships, Reinharts drive carriages, so it’s only House Veltras to compete with.”
Desinee El was truly a different kind of [Lady]. She’d gone from screaming and hollering for her life to relatively composed, if sweaty, in moments. She exhaled.
“Cattle Baron Marvus Lischelle? Do you have anywhere I might stay? And I do have a horse over there. If you would excuse me, I believe I need to find somewhere to pass out. Then may we sort out any issues later?”
“Er, of course, Lady Desinee. Aronia will put you up herself in our finest guest—Burlise, go tell your mother. May I ask, Lady Desinee, what Skill let you do all that?”
Desinee El smiled around.
“Oh, just…[Wild Rider]. Aunt Maviola would give you a protection amulet and make us steal a horse and ride it all the way back to the manor on her birthday, then pay for it. She did it for over two decades before she got bored. The one who stole the angriest horse got to double their allowance for the month.”
Desinee put her handkerchief away, then delicately swooned onto her horse’s saddle. And the [Ranchers] realized, for a second, that the haughty Five Families might still be all that in the north, but not quite what they expected.
 
——
 
The adventures of Desinee El in the Cowpat Plains were a small moment in the timeline of events occurring there. Minute, but Calidus Reinhart appreciated it.
Especially because the [Assassins] he’d sent to investigate what the Wall Lord was doing had gotten a very nice recording of Desinee riding the giant bull.
“I heard the House of El was good at that, but dead gods! Or living. Why do we say that? Is she married? Damn. Happily married?”
Life was full of disappointments. However, Calidus was entertained enough by having the recording transferred to a crystal and labelling it ‘[Lady] rides bull nonstop for seven minutes’ and making a few copies.
Then he just gave them out and listed a few on the black market and waited to see who bought them. He didn’t even get in trouble for false advertising because the actual thing was so entertaining.
Though, come to think of it, were there recordings of…? There were plenty of famous news events, but Calidus wondered if he could start a collection of more, uh, interesting events. He already had the famous ‘two ladies versus one fish’ recording, but he started asking his contacts if they had anything more fun.
Anyways, where was he? He stopped showing Zeomtoril and Selys the recording of the bull and slapped his forehead.
“Right, I was wondering what was up! Lady Ulva’s bearing down on them, and she’s about to murder them all or—sorry, sorry. Back to work.”
He rushed off as Selys turned to Zeomtoril.
“Do we have a policy against being harassed while working?”
“Under Calidus? Hah! The man is a walking distraction.”
 
——
 
Calidus was busier than normal with his stunt against the Assassin’s Guild. His peers actually wanted to talk to him, and he was making a big show of bumbling around his ‘Anti-Assassin’s Guild’ project. He doubted it fooled Aunt Magnolia, but he hoped he was low on her list of people to kill.
Regis had cursed him out, of course, but Calidus had put his name out there, and so his grandfather couldn’t remove him. Plus, Regis had started this mess.
Making his name public had multiple advantages, for all the disadvantages, but Calidus’ attention was still on the dig site with Wall Lord Ilvriss.
Something’s up. Enchanter Hedault was seen in the camp, and he doesn’t work for fraud or anything less than the best. But even Grandfather thinks that Wall Lord Ilvriss hasn’t dug up anything yet…but Grandfather is probably just buying information from Salazsar. If the Wall Lord’s sitting on it…
“Have the Ranks enlist in the Wall Lord’s camp. Be on their best behavior—just do their jobs. Don’t report in, don’t record anything. Just work. Call it a vacation. One assumes it’s a vacation instead of killing other [Assassins]. I want more over there. Just a hunch.”
He gave Selys more orders, and she scowled at him.
“We haven’t beaten the other Guild, Calidus.”
“No, but we’ve made it the north’s problem. Any of our people who are fighting them will get killed as ‘their’ [Assassins]. And there are eyes on us, so why not put them somewhere no one’s watching? Make it sixty. Ah! No arguing. Super Guildmaster’s orders.”
He was quite pleased she’d made full [Guildmistress], but he was still the boss. Calidus saw Selys’ tail curling as she read reports around the Cowpat Plains.
“Ugh, Lyonette’s there, so maybe there is something to it. Are we stealing whatever it is?”
“Potentially…not a fan of the [Princess]? How attractive is she, scale of one to ten?”
“I’m not dignifying that with a comment. And she has two annoying children. I don’t know this area, but—okay, fine, I’ll keep it on my radar as well. Actually…Ancestors. They have monsters over there too. Not many adventurers.”
“The [Ranchers] deal with it themselves. Why? Anything interesting over there? Crelers?”
Selys was reading from a [Message] scroll and she winced.
“Nope. This just in: a pack of Demonsoul Skunks have appeared and are causing havoc. That’s…genuinely bad news. They’d better have a Gold-rank team around somewhere, and any smart Gold-ranker will bail on the job unless they’re all-ranged. You’d need Halrac or someone like him to handle them.”
Calidus’ lips moved.
“Demonsoul…that sounds rather too impressive to call a skunk, doesn’t it? I’m all for hyperbole, but that?”
Selys gave Calidus a semi-amused look as she pulled out a monster bestiary and flipped to the ‘D’ section.
“I’ve never run into one, but this actually does deserve the name. Let’s see if they have a picture. You know skunks?”
“Regular skunks? White stripe? Horrific smell? Don’t pet them? One of my cousins once charmed one to look like a regular cat and let it loose in the backyard of a party.”
“…Do you have any normal stories about your family?”
“Only to me.”
Selys sighed.
“Right, right. Well, good. You know skunks. Imagine a skunk.”
“Very well.”
“A Demonsoul Skunk is nothing like that. Imagine a racoon. Now, imagine if you had six racoons. Now, imagine a skunk that big. With carnivore’s teeth. And the smell? It’s way worse than a regular skunk, and I’m told it lasts a lifetime, potentially, without remedy. Last thing…Demonsoul Skunks enjoy spraying people.”
“I see. And what are you doing for dinner tonight?”
“Do you ever get tired of asking?”
“Only if you tell me to stop.”
 
——
 
Skunks were just…skunks. Sure, they’d spray you, but it was a self-defense mechanism. Most would rather run away than actually need to spray. They were defensive omnivores that ate insects, rodents, lizards—even bird eggs.
They were, again aside from the terror they inspired due to their only weapon, arguably net positives. Some daring people even made pets out of them.
That was what a skunk was. Which was why there were [Naturalists], [Monster Hunters], adventurers, and related classes lobbying to classify the Demonsoul Skunk as a separate species. Or at least, one that had come from Rhir.
When Rabbo Golva had read all that and the lengthy warning from the Adventurer’s Guild, he’d put it down to claptrap. He’d told eight of his boys to ride out with bow and arrow and put the pack, or whatever it was, down. No need for adventurers. When six had come back, so foul-smelling that he nearly spewed his lunch when he got within a hundred feet of ‘em, raving about the skunks coming at them, big as hogs, eating the horses alive—
Then Rabbo Golva knew fear. And that was before he saw the Demonsoul Skunks.
They had red eyes, which stared out of their heads, rolling around, and when they bared their bloody teeth, you’d swear they were grinning. There was malice there. An empty monster staring out of an oversized skunk’s body with sharp dewclaws and…
And they were just evil.
Skunks could musk a target from fifteen feet away with accuracy. Demonsoul Skunks had a range of forty-five feet. They hit everything they saw.
Corusdeer who screamed and writhed. People on the roads. Even rabbits—puking, a kind of nausea from a smell that was part-magical that did not fade—
And then they killed. Or just—tortured?
They were at the herds. Rabbo Golva was watching, having ridden away from the pack. Only three dozen, but no one could tag them with arrows; they were fast, and they’d sprayed just as many [Ranchers]. The men and women were almost out of their minds with the stink, and no one dared get closer.
It would last years, apparently, even with treatment, before it fully faded. Who wanted…the animals wouldn’t get close. Horses reared, showing the whites of their eyes. But the cows?
“Dead gods.”
The Demonsoul Skunks had cornered some of the poor cattle. They sprayed first, and the animals tried to puke or just collapsed—then Gabbo saw a Demonsoul Skunk claw on top of a cow. It was smaller, but it began to rip into the cow’s back as the screaming animal writhed, spraying again, and then began to eat the cow from the back down. Bloody claws, and he swore—
It was grinning at him.
“Someone get an adventuring team out here. Now!”
“No one wants to take the contract, pa! They won’t take on Demonsouls! There’s a [Ranger] who says he’ll do it, but it’ll cost two hundred per head, and he’s six days away at his fastest!”
“Six?”
They’d kill entire herds by then. The Demonsouls had snuck up on the cattle in the long grass, and once they sprayed, the cows weren’t able to do more than puke. Rabbo was shouting.
“Get traps, get people with bows—keep ‘em away from our territory! Tell the other [Ranchers]!”
They’d pick off the skunks one by one. Though he’d been told they could kill even bulls by just stunning the bulls then eating their eyes—Rabbo was telling himself he had it under control.
Until he heard the chittering, like giggles, and saw the tall grass moving. Then he saw eight more groups winding through the grass and realized over a hundred were coming at him.
“Ride! Ride—”
 
——
 
Where had they all come from? A hundred? Marvus heard out the faintly-reeking son of Rabbo who’d come pelting towards him, wide-eyed and horrified.
“Four people dead, and how many cattle? Can’t you fight ‘em?”
“Your brain just shuts down ‘cause of the stink, Marvus! The Adventurer’s Guild in First Landing said—they said that there ain’t supposed to be that many. One pack of thirty, that’s normal. But nine?”
“First Landing’s Adventurer’s Guild is writin’ us?”
Marvus felt the back of his neck crawling, then the young Golva gasped.
“Marvus…someone said they thought they saw a half-Elf leading them this way a few days ago, but everyone laughed it off. A half-Elf with a flute, they said. Leadin’ ‘em like a [Piper] can do for rats. Only when one of the skunks went the other way, he walked over and stomped its brains in.”
“Hell’s own horses!”
Then Marvus saw it. He looked around, desperate.
“We’ve got a few longbows. Get nose plugs and—and traps, like Rabbo’s doing! If they’re coming this way, we can’t let them at the herds! They’ll foul the meat itself, and putting the cattle down would be a mercy!”
